Self World

Licences

Self World is free and open source under the BSD 3-Clause License. Parts of its starting world are derived from the original Self system, whose notices are reproduced below as its licence requires. Everything else in the builds is third-party software under permissive licences.

Every component is under a permissive licence (MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD, Zlib, ISC, Unicode-3.0, BSL-1.0, CC0, SIL OFL, Ubuntu Font Licence). None of them is copyleft, so all of them can be shipped together with Self World under BSD 3-Clause, provided these notices travel with the program; they do, in every download, and in every web app Self World exports.

The original Self system

Self World is an independent implementation written from scratch in Rust. It contains no source files from the original Self system, but some methods of its starting world (the protocol carried over from the original core world, in world.self) are adapted from the original Self source, so its copyright notices and disclaimers apply to those parts:

Self World
Copyright (c) 2026, Volodymyr Pavlyshyn
Licensed under the BSD 3-Clause License (see LICENSE).

Self World is an independent implementation of the Self programming language,
written from scratch in Rust. It contains no source files of the original Self
system. Some methods of its starting world (src/world.self, the section
"protocol from the original core world", and therefore also the JavaScript
translation of that world inside web apps that Self World exports) are adapted
from the source of the original Self system, objects/core in
https://github.com/russellallen/self. For those parts the original notices
apply; they are reproduced here unchanged, as the original licence requires.

Third-party software in the builds of Self World is listed, with the full
licence texts, in THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md (also at https://self-world.app/licenses/).

Other components

  • Rust standard library: MIT OR Apache-2.0; compiled into every build. https://github.com/rust-lang/rust
  • AppImage type 2 runtime: MIT (includes components under other licences, among them libfuse under LGPL-2.1-or-later); the start-up code inside the Linux .AppImage file; its complete source code is available from the repository. https://github.com/AppImage/type2-runtime
  • Inno Setup: Inno Setup License; builds the Windows installer. https://jrsoftware.org/isinfo.php
